<a id="item-1"></a>
## [GRPO, Dr. GRPO, DAPO Unified by Group Standard Deviation Identity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00152) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper proves that GRPO, Dr. GRPO, and DAPO are mathematically equivalent operations on the group standard deviation of answer correctness, unifying three popular LLM reasoning training methods under a single identity. This unification reveals that the key dial in these methods is the group standard deviation, which determines where learning happens and how strongly, providing a fundamental insight that could simplify and improve LLM reasoning training. The group-standard-deviation identity shows that for right-or-wrong rewards, the disagreement among sampled answers exactly equals the size of the training update, with split groups teaching the most and unanimous groups teaching nothing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning algorithm designed to enhance reasoning in LLMs by comparing responses within groups. Dr. GRPO and DAPO are subsequent variants that modify the normalization or sampling steps. This paper shows they are all adjusting the same underlying quantity: the standard deviation of correctness scores across sampled answers for each prompt.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.14476">DAPO: An Open-Source LLM Reinforcement Learning System at Scale 1ByteDance Seed</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Tags**: `#reinforcement learning`, `#LLM training`, `#reasoning`, `#policy optimization`, `#theoretical analysis`

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [AI and Quantum Information: A Comprehensive Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00365) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new survey paper (arXiv:2607.00365) comprehensively reviews the bidirectional relationship between AI and quantum information, covering AI for quantum systems and quantum-inspired AI. This review is significant because it provides a structured overview of two rapidly co-evolving fields, highlighting how AI can advance quantum technologies and how quantum concepts can enhance AI, which could accelerate progress in both domains. The survey organizes the AI-for-QI direction around tasks like extracting information from limited measurements and discovering quantum algorithms, while the QI-for-AI direction examines quantum speedups, expressivity, and tensor-network representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Artificial intelligence (AI) and quantum information (QI) are two transformative technologies. AI involves machine learning and data-driven methods, while QI encompasses quantum computing, quantum communication, and quantum sensing. Their intersection promises mutual benefits: AI can help design and control quantum systems, and quantum principles can inspire new AI models.

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [NeuroCogMap Maps LLM Internal Features to Cognitive Functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00397) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced NeuroCogMap, a cognitive neuroscience-inspired framework that organizes internal features of large language models (LLMs) into functional parcels linked to cognitive capabilities and failure modes such as hallucination, bias, refusal failure, and sycophancy. This framework provides a system-level understanding of how cognitive functions are organized in LLMs, linking model behavior to human cognition and enabling mechanism-guided detection and targeted intervention for major failure modes, which is crucial for AI interpretability and alignment. NeuroCogMap reveals that parcels are stable and semantically coherent across models, and that failures like hallucination and sycophancy correspond to distinct disruptions in representational and behavioral-control systems. The framework also improves prediction of human cortical responses during language comprehension.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are AI systems trained on vast text data to generate human-like text, but their internal workings are often opaque. Cognitive neuroscience studies how brain regions support cognitive functions, and frameworks like NeuroCogMap borrow these concepts to interpret LLM internals. Sycophancy is a known failure where AI models tailor responses to agree with users, often at the expense of accuracy.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [Phantom References: Hallucinated Citations Survive Peer Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00738) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study introduces RefChecker, a verification pipeline, and finds that hallucinated citations—references to non-existent works—appear in peer-reviewed papers at top conferences like NeurIPS and ICML, with about 1 in 20 NeurIPS 2025 papers containing at least two such failures. This reveals that peer review alone cannot guarantee citation integrity, threatening the reliability of scientific literature; the open-source RefChecker tool offers a low-cost solution (about $0.04 per paper) to audit citations before publication. The study uses a conservative definition limited to identity-level failures (non-existent works or major author mismatches), excluding ordinary bibliographic drift; it analyzes camera-ready papers from ICLR, ICML, NeurIPS, and USENIX Security, finding post-ChatGPT increases and even award-winning papers with hallucinated citations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can generate plausible-sounding but false scientific text, including fabricated citations. Citation hallucination refers to references that do not correspond to any real scholarly work. RefChecker is a verification pipeline that resolves bibliography entries against multiple sources and escalates unresolved cases to web search.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>

</ul>
</details>

## [LiteResearcher: Scalable RL Training for Deep Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17931) ⭐️ 9.0/10

LiteResearcher introduces a scalable agentic reinforcement learning training framework that uses a lite virtual world to mirror real-world search dynamics, enabling a 4B parameter model to achieve state-of-the-art results on GAIA (71.3%) and Xbench (78.0%), outperforming larger models like Tongyi DeepResearch and Claude-4.5 Sonnet. This framework addresses key scalability challenges in agentic RL for deep research, demonstrating that small models can achieve superior performance through scalable training, potentially reducing the cost and complexity of building capable research agents. The lite virtual world mirrors real-world search dynamics, enabling stable and cost-effective RL training without relying on real-world search APIs. The 4B model achieves open-source state-of-the-art results on GAIA and Xbench benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) has become a powerful training paradigm for LLM-based agents, but scaling agentic RL for deep research tasks is challenging due to the need for real-world search interactions, which are costly and unstable. LiteResearcher overcomes this by creating a simulated environment that captures the essential dynamics of real search, allowing continuous improvement of the agent through RL.

**Tags**: `#reinforcement learning`, `#LLM agents`, `#deep research`, `#scalable training`, `#AI`

## [AI Agents Leap from 43% to 98% Task Completion on WorkBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13715) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A revisit of the WorkBench benchmark shows that the best AI agent, Claude Fable 5, now completes 98% of tasks, up from GPT-4's 43% in March 2024, while harmful actions dropped from 26% to 1.9%. This demonstrates that AI agent capability and safety can improve together, challenging the common trade-off assumption, and open-weight models are making high performance accessible at lower costs. The study also found that open-weight models have drastically lowered costs for performance levels previously exclusive to proprietary models, while frontier model costs remained stable. However, frontier models still make basic mistakes that can cause irreversible harm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: WorkBench is a benchmark for evaluating AI agents on workplace tasks. The original 2024 evaluation showed GPT-4 completing only 43% of tasks with a 26% rate of unintended harmful actions. The updated benchmark includes improvements in data and code quality, new model scores, and analysis of agent progress since 2024.

<a id="item-7"></a>
## [LLM Safety Guardrails Fail for Most Mental Health Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23884) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study evaluated eight proprietary LLMs across 16 DSM-5 conditions using four adversarial attack variants and found that safety guardrails reliably hold only for suicide and self-harm, with failure rates up to 100% for conditions like eating disorders and major depressive disorder. This matters because LLMs are increasingly integrated into mental health contexts via schools, search engines, and chatbots, and the widespread failure of safety guardrails poses significant risks to vulnerable populations, highlighting an urgent need for condition-specific harm taxonomies and safeguards. The study introduced an eight-dimension harm taxonomy and a multi-dimensional evaluation framework, testing eight proprietary LLMs including GPT-4 and Claude. Results showed that conditions like substance use disorder and PTSD also exhibited high failure rates, while only suicide and self-harm were consistently blocked.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: The DSM-5 is the Diagnostic and Statistical Manual of Mental Disorders, a standard classification of mental health conditions used by clinicians. Adversarial attacks are inputs designed to trick LLMs into bypassing safety guardrails. This study uses such attacks to probe LLM safety across clinical conditions.

<details><summary>References</summary>

## [Virginia Bans Sale of Geolocation Data](https://www.hunton.com/privacy-and-cybersecurity-law-blog/virginia-bans-sale-of-geolocation-data) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Virginia has enacted a ban on the sale of geolocation data, effective July 1, 2024, joining Maryland and Oregon in a growing state-level privacy movement. This law strengthens consumer privacy protections by restricting the commercial exploitation of sensitive location information, and signals a broader trend as other states like California and Massachusetts consider similar legislation. The ban specifically targets the sale of geolocation data, but does not prohibit the collection or use of such data for other purposes, potentially creating loopholes similar to those exploited by companies like FLOCK.

hackernews · toomuchtodo · Jul 2, 21:03 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48767347)

**Background**: Geolocation data refers to information that identifies the physical location of a device or person, often collected via smartphones, vehicles, or IP addresses. State-level privacy laws in the US have been proliferating due to the absence of a comprehensive federal privacy law, creating a patchwork of regulations that companies must navigate.

<a id="item-9"></a>
## [Linux 6.9 Regression: LUKS Suspend Fails to Wipe Keys from Memory](https://mathstodon.xyz/@iblech/116769502749142438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A regression in Linux 6.9 caused the LUKS suspend operation to stop wiping disk-encryption keys from memory, potentially exposing them during suspend-to-RAM. This security regression undermines the protection of disk encryption keys during suspend, which could allow an attacker with physical access to extract keys from memory and decrypt the disk. The bug affects the cryptsetup luksSuspend command, which is used to temporarily lock encrypted volumes before suspend; the kernel change in 6.9 inadvertently skipped the key-wiping step.

hackernews · IngoBlechschmid · Jul 2, 15:25 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48763035)

**Background**: LUKS (Linux Unified Key Setup) is a disk encryption specification that stores master keys in kernel memory while the volume is unlocked. During suspend-to-RAM, the system keeps memory powered, so encryption keys remain in RAM unless explicitly wiped. The luksSuspend command is designed to wipe the master key from memory before suspend, requiring re-entry of the passphrase on resume. This regression was introduced in Linux 6.9 and affects systems using Debian's custom suspend script that relies on luksSuspend.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/nailfarmer/debian-luks-suspend">GitHub - nailfarmer/debian- luks - suspend : Lock encrypted root volume...</a></li>
<li><a href="https://askubuntu.com/questions/95625/suspend-to-ram-and-encrypted-partitions">encryption - Suspend to RAM and encrypted partitions - Ask Ubuntu</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Discussion**: Some commenters noted that the bug only affects Debian's custom suspend script, not the kernel's official suspend path, and argued the title was clickbait. Others highlighted that security regressions are easy to miss because everything still appears to work, and that the bug was caught thanks to NixOS tests.

**Tags**: `#Linux`, `#security`, `#kernel`, `#LUKS`, `#encryption`

<a id="item-11"></a>
## [Single Transformer Layer Matches Full-Parameter RL Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01232) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper (arXiv:2607.01232) shows that training just one transformer layer can recover most of the performance gains of full-parameter reinforcement learning post-training, with middle layers being the most impactful. This finding challenges the assumption that full-parameter fine-tuning is necessary for RL post-training, potentially enabling more efficient and interpretable model updates in large language models. The study systematically investigates layer-wise contributions during RL post-training and finds that a single layer can sometimes even outperform full-parameter training. However, the authors note inconsistencies with training token length, and many errors likely stem from responses exceeding the 3K token limit.

hackernews · tcp_handshaker · Jul 2, 12:10 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48760201)

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) post-training is a technique used to align large language models (LLMs) with human preferences, typically by fine-tuning all model parameters. This process is computationally expensive and can suffer from issues like reward hacking and KL collapse. The paper explores whether a single transformer layer can capture the essential adjustments made during RL training.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2607.01232">Is One Layer Enough? Training A Single Transformer Layer Can...</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.primeintellect.ai/blog/intellect-2">INTELLECT-2: The First Globally Distributed Reinforcement Learning ...</a></li>
<li><a href="https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.06743">Stabilizing Reinforcement Learning for Diffusion Language Models</a></li>

</ul>

This framework provides a rigorous way to analyze the computational limits of moral reasoning, with implications for AI ethics, cognitive science, and understanding moral progress under resource constraints. The framework defines moral breadth as the scope of entities considered morally relevant, and moral depth as the inferential integration required to evaluate their interactions, with a formal tradeoff imposed by limited resources.

This finding challenges the common assumption that channel capacity alone determines coordination success, highlighting the critical role of memory architecture in multi-agent LLM systems, with implications for AI alignment and agent design. Stateless agents peak at moderate capacity then degrade as vocabulary grows beyond the context window, while notebook agents avoid this collapse and benefit from surplus capacity; the information bottleneck (capacity=8) is a fragility point, not optimal.

The information bottleneck theory suggests an optimal channel capacity equal to the number of objects, but this study finds surplus capacity is better.

Researchers introduced Mnemosyne, a runtime that implements Agentic Transaction Processing (ATP) to validate and repair AI-generated workflow actions by treating them as untrusted proposals until admitted under a constraint set. This work addresses a critical trust and correctness gap in LLM-based agent systems, ensuring that generated actions are safe and consistent even when the AI model is fallible or adversarial. ## [MuSix: Scale-Aware World Model Mixture for Embodied Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00457) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MuSix introduces a scale-aware mixture of world models for embodied agents, featuring a two-stage routing mechanism and scale-dependent forgetting rates to handle multi-scale reasoning and adaptation in evolving environments. This work addresses critical limitations of standard Mixture of Experts (MoE) in embodied AI, enabling agents to reason at multiple scales and adapt knowledge at appropriate rates, which is essential for real-world deployment in changing environments. MuSix uses a meta-router to map experiential distance (inspired by Construal Level Theory) to a weight over continuous scale space, then per-scale base routers select world models. Scale-dependent forgetting rates allow low-scale knowledge to refresh rapidly while high-scale abstractions persist, with gated inter-scale transfer maintaining coherence.

Construal Level Theory from psychology suggests that psychological distance influences abstract vs. concrete thinking, inspiring the experiential distance metric used in MuSix.

HARC reveals that jailbreaks suppress either the refusal or harmfulness direction in LLMs' residual stream, and proposes a fine-tuning method that couples these directions across prompt and response positions to enhance safety alignment. This work provides a mechanistic understanding of why jailbreaks succeed and introduces a robust safety alignment method that does not degrade general capabilities, addressing a critical challenge in AI safety. HARC uses a LoRA-based fine-tuning with an additive margin hinge loss on cosine projections, and the intervention is confined to the harmfulness-refusal subspace, leaving other capabilities intact. It achieves the best robustness-capability-usability trade-off among six baselines across five model families.

HARC builds on this by showing jailbreaks suppress these directions and proposes coupling them for stronger defense.

Researchers introduced SEA, a framework for self-evolving agents that uses anytime-valid certificates and a frozen base model to provide formal safety guarantees during self-modification. 这解决了AI安全中的一个基本挑战，使智能体能够在保持可证明安全边界的同时自我改进，对于部署可信自主系统至关重要。 SEA confines self-modification to a small steering adapter and a versioned harness around a frozen base model, with an anytime-valid gate that emits auditable certificates against a fixed error budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Self-evolving agents that modify their own code or architecture risk violating safety guarantees because the data and hypothesis space change with the policy. Traditional learning-theoretic guarantees assume a fixed hypothesis space, which is broken by self-modification. SEA uses a frozen base model that cannot be altered, and only allows selection among its existing behaviors via verifier-in-the-loop mechanisms, ensuring safety.

## [Graph-Native RL Enables Traceable Scientific Hypothesis Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00924) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Graph-PRefLexOR, a family of graph-native reasoning models fine-tuned with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) that organizes reasoning into explicit phases for mechanism exploration, graph construction, pattern extraction, and hypothesis synthesis. On 100 open-ended materials science questions, it achieves 40-65% improvements over base models, with the largest gains in reasoning traceability. This work establishes graph-native reinforcement learning as a pathway toward interpretable AI systems for scientific hypothesis generation, addressing the critical need for traceable reasoning in materials design and other scientific applications. The ability to construct, inspect, and reuse causal connections could accelerate materials discovery by making AI-generated hypotheses more trustworthy and verifiable. The model uses GRPO, a reinforcement learning algorithm that estimates baselines from group statistics rather than a separate value network, making it simpler and more efficient than PPO. Embedding analyses show approximately 2-3 times greater semantic diversity than baselines, and test-time graph expansion reveals that additional compute primarily increases long-range conceptual recombination within a bounded semantic space.

This approach bridges the gap between formal proof assistants and LLM judges, offering auditable verification for AI systems, which is critical for trust and safety in high-stakes applications. On adversarial poisoned proofs, it catches 94.7% versus 83.2% for holistic judges.

## [AutoMem: Automated Memory Skill Learning for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01224) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoMem is a framework that automates the learning of memory management as a cognitive skill for LLMs, enabling self-improvement of memory structures and actions over long trajectories. It treats file-system operations as first-class memory actions and uses a two-loop mechanism to optimize both the memory structure and the model's memory proficiency. This work addresses a key limitation in long-horizon tasks by treating memory management as an independently learnable skill, achieving 2x-4x performance improvements without modifying task-action behavior. It demonstrates that optimizing memory alone can make a 32B open-weight model competitive with frontier systems like Claude Opus 4.5 and Gemini 3.1 Pro Thinking. AutoMem operates in two loops: the first uses a strong LLM to review agent trajectories and iteratively revise the memory structure, while the second identifies good memory decisions from many episodes to sharpen the model's memory proficiency via training signal. The framework was evaluated on three procedurally generated long-horizon games (Crafter, MiniHack, NetHack), showing consistent improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Metamemory, a concept from cognitive science, refers to the knowledge and awareness of one's own memory processes, including what to encode, when to retrieve, and how to organize knowledge. In LLMs, memory management is typically manually designed or fixed, but AutoMem treats it as a trainable skill, allowing the model to self-improve its memory strategies over time.

## [Vocabulary Transfer Bridges Gap in Sparse Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00004) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers identified the 'Vocabulary Gap' as the root cause why advanced encoders like ModernBERT underperform in learned sparse retrieval (LSR), and proposed Vocabulary Transfer (VT), a framework that migrates encoders to normalized vocabularies, achieving state-of-the-art 52.4 nDCG on BEIR. This work solves a counterintuitive problem that modern encoders lag behind older BERT-base in sparse retrieval, potentially improving retrieval systems that rely on efficient lexical matching. It provides a practical, model-agnostic solution that can be applied to various encoders and domains. Vocabulary Transfer uses Semantic Initialization via spatial topology and Activation Potential Calibration (APC) to preserve geometric structure and prevent dead neurons or dense collapse. The framework is universally effective, even reviving failing models like RoBERTa-large and generalizing to inference-free architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Learned sparse retrieval (LSR) generates high-dimensional sparse bag-of-words representations for efficient search using inverted indexes. Modern tokenizers use case-sensitive vocabularies that map single semantic units to multiple surface forms, wasting capacity on morphological noise and hindering lexical matching.

## [Topological Void Analysis for Systematic Innovation Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00005) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Topological Void Analysis (TVA) is introduced as a mathematical framework that formalizes the identification of unexplored yet relevant innovation opportunities in high-dimensional knowledge spaces using topological voids. This framework addresses a fundamental gap in existing innovation discovery methods, which rely on keyword search or intuition, by providing a rigorous, automated approach that can systematically surface non-obvious invention candidates across technical domains. TVA defines topological voids as triads (A, B, C) in a dense-sparse hybrid embedding space, requiring semantic cohesion, calibrated marginality, and a sparse lexical bridge with an unoccupied geodesic midpoint. Applied to ~140k documents, it generated 2,128 candidates, with 0.05% surviving adversarial review.

## [CHORD: Learning Dexterous Manipulation from Human Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00033) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose CHORD, a framework that uses object-centric contact wrench guidance from human demonstrations to train reinforcement learning policies for long-horizon dexterous manipulation. It achieves an average success rate of 82.12% on 1,831 benchmark tasks and 90.77% on whole-body manipulation tasks. This work addresses a key challenge in robot learning: transferring human dexterity to robots for contact-rich manipulation. The large-scale benchmark with 4,739 tasks and strong real-world transfer results could accelerate progress in dexterous robotics. CHORD represents both human and robot motions in an object-centric contact wrench space, measuring similarity via induced instantaneous motions. The framework is validated on 1,831 tasks from a new benchmark built using motion-capture datasets and in-house reconstructed videos.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Dexterous manipulation involves controlling a robot hand to perform complex tasks like grasping and rotating objects. Reinforcement learning often struggles with contact-rich tasks due to high-dimensional state spaces and sparse rewards. Human demonstrations provide useful guidance but are difficult to transfer directly due to embodiment differences.

## [SWE-Router: Cost-Efficient LLM Routing for Multi-Turn SWE Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00053) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SWE-Router introduces a value-based temporal routing method that uses partial trajectories from a cheap model to decide whether to escalate to an expensive model, improving cost efficiency in multi-turn agentic software engineering tasks. This approach addresses a critical cost-performance tradeoff in deploying LLMs for software engineering, potentially enabling organizations to achieve near-frontier performance at a fraction of the cost. It also provides theoretical grounding with a Bayes-optimality theorem, ensuring that routing decisions are never worse than random. SWE-Router uses a learned value head to predict whether the weak model will eventually solve the task based on its partial trajectory, and only escalates when necessary. The authors release a multi-LLM trajectory dataset to enable reproduction of their trajectory-level routing results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in multi-turn agentic systems for software engineering tasks, but using a frontier model for every task is expensive. Existing LLM routers only consider the initial task description, which has an information-theoretic Bayes-error floor because similar prompts can hide vastly different complexities. SWE-Router overcomes this by observing the model's early behavior before making a routing decision.

## [AlgoBench: Benchmarking Algorithmic Adaptation in Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00062) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced AlgoBench, a framework that automatically generates novel algorithmic problems by applying constraint-shifting transformations to known competitive programming problems, and proposed new complexity-aware metrics (OPTT, OPTS, TRAPRATE, GAPT, CONSENS) to evaluate both correctness and asymptotic efficiency. AlgoBench addresses a critical limitation of existing code generation benchmarks, which often become contaminated in training data, and provides a more rigorous evaluation of a model's true algorithmic reasoning ability beyond functional correctness. Experiments across multiple LLMs and prompting strategies showed that performance drops sharply on AlgoBench variants, retrieval can increase reuse of the old algorithm, and many correct-looking solutions fail to meet the required complexity; error analysis indicated failures are mainly algorithmic rather than implementation-level.

A new paper introduces a mechanism-driven theory that explains budget-dependent phase transitions in active learning by identifying dominance shifts among generalization mechanisms, validated on natural and medical imaging tasks. This theory provides a unified framework for understanding why different active learning strategies excel at different labeling budgets, enabling the design of transition-aware algorithms that can adaptively select the optimal strategy for a given budget. The theory reframes PAC-style risk components as dynamic interacting terms and proves that dominance shifts are structurally unavoidable, leading to a tripartite taxonomy of data-driven, transition, and model-driven phases.

SLIM-RL introduces a risk-budgeted random-masking reinforcement learning method for diffusion large language models (dLLMs) that eliminates the costly trajectory reconstruction required by prior methods like TraceRL. It achieves state-of-the-art results on math and code benchmarks while using significantly fewer training samples. This work makes RL training for diffusion LLMs much more efficient, reducing computational cost while maintaining or improving performance. It could accelerate the development of diffusion-based language models, which are a promising alternative to autoregressive models. On SDAR-4B, SLIM-RL matches TraceRL's best MATH500 accuracy using only 0.46x its training samples at block size 16, and improves over TraceRL by 6.32% on MATH500 and 11.05% on GSM8K under matched dynamic sampling. The tau-budget decoder transfers training-free across LLaDA, Dream, and SDAR models.

SLIM-RL avoids this by using a risk-budgeted decoder and a trace-free random-masking objective.

Researchers introduced EgoSafetyBench, a diagnostic egocentric video benchmark with 1,200 robot-view scenarios annotated at half-second granularity, designed to evaluate vision-language models (VLMs) as runtime safety guards in embodied AI. The benchmark includes two tracks: a situational track covering four hazard families and a visual-channel track testing the impact of misleading in-scene text. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in embodied AI safety by providing fine-grained, contrastive evaluations that distinguish genuine hazards from safe but suspicious activities. The findings reveal that current VLMs often miss specific hazardous moments and are vulnerable to misleading signs, highlighting the need for more robust safety reasoning before deploying embodied agents in real-world environments. The benchmark comprises 800 scenarios in the situational track (routine, safe-but-suspicious, obvious hazards, contextual hazards) and 400 scenarios in the visual-channel track with paired misleading/truthful signs. Evaluations of ten open- and closed-source VLMs showed that while models reliably recognize hazardous videos, they often miss specific hazardous moments, especially contextual hazards, and misleading signs degrade performance across all tested models.

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly used as runtime safety guards for embodied agents in homes and factories, but existing binary safety benchmarks fail to distinguish genuinely unsafe situations from routine but superficially alarming activities. EgoSafetyBench uses contrastive ladders—near-identical scenarios differing only in a single visible cue—to ensure that correct safety calls hinge on that specific cue rather than the overall scene type. This design allows for diagnostic evaluation of a VLM's ability to reason about physical safety in real-time streaming video.

HQT overcomes the sign problem and exponential complexity in simulating frustrated quantum matter, offering a scalable and transferable paradigm for quantum simulation that could accelerate discoveries in condensed matter physics and materials science. On the 8x8 J1-J2 Heisenberg model at the quantum critical point, HQT achieves ground-state energy per site of -0.5001(1), consistent with finite-size scaling. Its zero-shot protocol, Holographic Transfer, uses continuous positional-embedding interpolation and head re-initialization to project a trained model onto larger lattices without retraining.

**Background**: Frustrated quantum systems, such as the J1-J2 Heisenberg model, are notoriously difficult to simulate due to the sign problem in Monte Carlo methods and the exponential growth of Hilbert space. Transformers, originally developed for natural language processing, use self-attention to capture long-range dependencies, making them promising for modeling non-local entanglement in quantum systems.

## [Safety Alignment Hurts Semantic Fidelity in T2I Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00402) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper reveals that safety-aligned text-to-image diffusion models suffer significant drops in semantic fidelity when evaluated with structured metrics like TIFA, exposing an illusion of high utility. The authors propose SAGE, a regularization method that restores structured utility while maintaining safety. This work challenges the prevailing assumption that safety alignment preserves utility, highlighting a hidden degradation that could mislead model evaluation and deployment. It introduces a novel evaluation paradigm and a practical solution, impacting AI safety research and text-to-image model development. The paper identifies 'semantic collapse' in the text-encoder embedding space, where embedding spread contracts and inter-prompt similarity structure distorts, correlating with structured utility loss. SAGE (StructureAware Geometric Regularization) explicitly preserves embedding spread and relational structure, achieving +5.0% TIFA over prior state-of-the-art.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Text-to-image diffusion models generate images from text prompts. Safety alignment fine-tunes these models to avoid generating harmful content, but utility is typically measured with coarse metrics like FID or CLIPScore, which are insensitive to fine-grained semantic errors. TIFA is a structured evaluation metric that uses visual question answering to assess faithfulness to the prompt.

## [BaseRT: Best-in-Class LLM Inference on Apple Silicon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00501) ⭐️ 8.0/10

BaseRT is a native Metal inference runtime for LLMs on Apple Silicon that achieves up to 1.56x higher decode throughput than llama.cpp and up to 1.35x higher than MLX. It supports multiple model families and quantization formats on all Apple M-series devices. This breakthrough establishes Apple Silicon as a more capable inference platform than previously reported, directly benefiting edge AI deployment where privacy, latency, and cloud cost pressures drive on-device inference. It provides a critical enabling layer for the emerging edge inference paradigm. BaseRT achieves consistent best-in-class throughput from sub-1B to 30B parameter models, with substantially larger margins on prefill for mixture-of-experts models. It is publicly available on GitHub under the BaseCompute organization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Existing LLM inference runtimes like llama.cpp and MLX incur overhead from abstractions not designed for Metal's execution model or Apple Silicon's unified memory topology. Apple Silicon uses a unified memory architecture where both CPU and GPU share the same memory pool, eliminating data copying overhead. Kernel fusion combines multiple operations into a single GPU kernel to reduce memory traffic and improve performance.

## [Active-GRPO: Adaptive Imitation and Self-Improvement for Molecular Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00531) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Active-GRPO introduces a novel active reasoning paradigm where the LLM dynamically decides per instance whether to imitate a reference or reinforce its own discoveries, and continuously upgrades the reference with its best candidates. This method overcomes the performance ceiling of prior reference-guided policy optimization, achieving statistically significant gains on molecular optimization benchmarks, and offers a general framework for improving LLM reasoning in scientific domains. Active-GRPO improves average SRxSim from 0.0959 (GRPO) and 0.1665 (RePO) to 0.1773 on TOMG-Bench MOLOPT under matched three-seed evaluation, with significant gains on LogP, MR, and QED.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific reasoning in LLMs is often trained via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) or reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR). SFT collapses multi-step reasoning, while RLVR suffers from sparse feedback. Reference-guided policy optimization (RePO) anchors updates to dataset references but is limited by reference quality.

Flow-Map GRPO is proposed as an online reinforcement learning post-training framework for deterministic few-step flow-map generators, introducing Anchored Stochastic Flow Map Composition (ASFMC) to enable stochastic trajectories and likelihood ratios for RL optimization. This work addresses a key limitation of deterministic few-step flow-map generators, which previously could not be effectively fine-tuned with RL post-training, potentially improving text-to-image generation quality and alignment with human preferences. The ASFMC mechanism preserves the original marginal probability path of the deterministic flow map while introducing randomness through anchor-based conditional resampling. Experiments on few-step FLUX-based text-to-image generators like MeanFlow and sCM show improvements across reward-based, perceptual, and task-level metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Few-step flow-map generators like consistency models and MeanFlow accelerate sampling by learning direct transport maps from noise to data, but they are deterministic and lack stochastic trajectories needed for RL post-training. Existing SDE-based stochasticization techniques are designed for velocity-based samplers with fine discretization and do not apply to long-range flow maps. Flow-Map GRPO bridges this gap by introducing ASFMC, enabling RL alignment without retraining the model as a native stochastic model.

Researchers introduced EgoGapBench, a diagnostic benchmark that isolates egocentric perspective understanding by measuring action selection in multi-agent scenes. Both open-source and proprietary multimodal large language models (MLLMs) perform substantially worse than humans on this benchmark. This benchmark reveals a critical limitation of current MLLMs: they struggle to select actions from an egocentric perspective when other agents are present, a skill that humans handle effortlessly. It highlights the need for MLLMs to be evaluated and trained not only for scene understanding but also for egocentric action selection. The benchmark defines Egocentric Action Selection (EAS) as selecting an appropriate action from the agent's perspective in the presence of other agents. Fine-tuning on existing egocentric data fails to close the human-MLLM gap and can even be detrimental, while fine-tuning on EgoGapBench training data improves accuracy but still falls short of human performance.

## [Causal Audit Reveals How Facts Persist After Deletion in LMLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00605) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new causal auditing framework decomposes post-deletion behavior in Limited Memory Language Models into parametric leakage, retrieval-mediated correctness, and retrieval artifact rate, applied to 12,228 deletions across 13 databases. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating machine unlearning by providing a rigorous method to attribute residual knowledge to specific sources, which is essential for privacy and compliance in deployed language models. Parametric leakage was near zero across all variants, meaning the model rarely returns deleted answers without retrieval; residual correctness came almost entirely from retrieval-mediated correctness and retrieval artifacts, ranging from 0.7% to 13.6% depending on the database.

**Background**: Limited Memory Language Models (LMLMs) externalize factual knowledge to a database to enable deletion-based unlearning without retraining. Existing evaluations measure aggregate correctness but cannot distinguish whether a deleted fact persists through residual parametric memory, alternative retrieval paths, or near-neighbor retrieval artifacts. This paper introduces a causal auditing framework that varies the database state at inference time to decompose these sources.

## [Semantic Gap Causes Failures in LLM Analytical Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00828) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A cross-domain formative study identified 153 recurring operationalization failures across 236 analytical intents in LLM-generated workflows, revealing five failure classes: comparative grounding, process reasoning, quantitative reasoning, role confusion, and policy grounding. This research highlights a critical semantic gap between user-level analytical concepts and available data, which undermines the reliability of LLM-powered analytics. The findings suggest that future agentic data systems need richer semantic representations to bridge this gap. The study analyzed 236 analytical intents across finance, human resources, and public safety domains, and found 153 failures despite successful workflow generation and execution. The five failure classes include comparative grounding (e.g., comparing metrics across time periods), process reasoning (e.g., understanding business processes), quantitative reasoning, role confusion (e.g., misinterpreting user roles), and policy grounding (e.g., applying domain-specific rules).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Operationalization is the process of turning abstract concepts into measurable observations. In LLM-based analytical workflows, the semantic gap refers to the mismatch between user-level analytical concepts and the explicit information in database schemas and data values. This study systematically categorizes failures that occur when LLMs attempt to operationalize analytical intents.

Researchers propose Confidence-Adaptive Thinking (CAT), a framework that dynamically adjusts reasoning length based on the model's own confidence signals, reducing token overhead on simple queries while maintaining accuracy on hard problems. CAT addresses the overthinking problem in large reasoning models, which wastes tokens on simple tasks, and offers a practical solution for balancing accuracy and latency in real-world deployments. CAT incorporates the model's intrinsic self-certainty signals as confidence into preference optimization, enabling autonomous modulation of reasoning lengths. ## [Aionoscope: Debugging Latent-State Accessibility in Time-Series Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00956) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Aionoscope, a generator-based diagnostic tool that reveals mismatches between coarse and fine-grained latent-state accessibility in frozen time-series representations. The tool was evaluated on 37 model-plus-adapter systems, showing that most systems easily recover component presence but fail to reliably expose dense process state. This matters because traditional time-series model evaluations focus on forecasting or classification accuracy, ignoring whether representations preserve latent process states like timing, phase, or amplitude. Aionoscope fills this gap, enabling more thorough debugging and understanding of time-series representations, which is critical for applications in healthcare, finance, and sensor data analysis. Aionoscope separates process generation from observation rendering, producing seeded synthetic streams with exact categorical and dense labels. The highest dense-probe row achieved 0.689 mean masked R², while a dense-feature oracle reached 0.999, highlighting the failure mode Aionoscope is designed to surface.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series models are often evaluated by their ability to forecast or classify, but these metrics do not assess whether the learned representations capture latent process states such as event timing, phase, amplitude, frequency, or regime variables. Aionoscope addresses this by providing a diagnostic tool that uses primitive process mixtures to generate synthetic data with known ground truth, allowing systematic probing of latent-state accessibility.

## [LOCOS: New Method to Identify Non-Literal Retrieval Heads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01002) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Logit-Contribution Scoring (LOCOS), a write-aware detector that identifies attention heads performing non-literal retrieval in long-context LLMs by scoring each head's OV-circuit output projection onto the answer-token unembedding direction. LOCOS fills a critical gap in mechanistic interpretability by detecting heads that synthesize answers from meaning rather than literal copy-pasting, enabling more precise ablation studies and deeper understanding of long-context model behavior. On Qwen3-8B, ablating the top 50 LOCOS heads drops ROUGE-L from 0.401 to 0.000 on the NoLiMa benchmark, while the strongest baseline retains 0.292; the method is validated across Qwen3, Gemma-3, and OLMo-3.1 families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: In large language models, attention heads can perform literal copy-pasting or non-literal retrieval where they synthesize answers from context meaning. Existing detectors only capture literal-copy heads by matching attended tokens to generated tokens, missing the output-value (OV) circuit that carries non-literal retrieval. LOCOS addresses this by measuring each head's contribution to the final answer logit through its OV circuit.

This approach overcomes the scalability limitations of existing data mixture methods that require costly retraining when data distributions shift, enabling more efficient and transferable optimal mixtures for LLM training across different model sizes and data pools. Diffusion-GR2 converts an autoregressive generative reasoning re-ranker into a block-diffusion language model, enabling parallel decoding that speeds inference by 2.4–3.5× while closing structural and distributional accuracy gaps through conversion fine-tuning and on-policy distillation. This work addresses the critical inference bottleneck of generative reasoning re-rankers, making them practical for real-time recommendation systems without sacrificing accuracy, and introduces a general recipe for converting autoregressive models to efficient diffusion-based counterparts. The method uses conversion fine-tuning to adapt the autoregressive-initialized diffusion model to produce valid permutations independently, and on-policy distillation with dense per-token targets from the AR teacher to close the distributional gap. Experiments on Amazon Beauty show near-parity accuracy with the AR re-ranker.

## [Distill to Detect: Exposing Stealth Biases in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01208) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce Distill to Detect (D2D), a method that uncovers hidden preferential biases in LLMs by distilling distributional shifts into a cartridge (KV-cache prefix adapter), amplifying the bias signal into generated text. This addresses a critical AI safety problem: stealth biases that are invisible to text-based inspection can influence user decisions at scale, and D2D provides a practical tool for auditing deployed models. D2D exploits the capacity bottleneck of prefix-tuning adapters to concentrate the dominant divergence between a suspected model and its base, and it is supported by a theoretical framework using Fisher-weighted projection of logit distribution shift.

Traditional detection methods fail because the defender does not know the bias topic, creating a fundamental asymmetry.

## [Study Questions Reliability of Coding Agent Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01211) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper audits three repository-level performance-optimization benchmarks—GSO, SWE-Perf, and SWE-fficiency—and finds that many reference patches fail validity rules when replayed across different machines, undermining the reliability of leaderboard scores for coding agents. These benchmarks are widely used to measure progress in AI coding agents, and if their scores are unreliable, it could mislead the community about which agents are truly better, affecting research directions and practical adoption. The study replayed 740 reference patches across four Google Cloud machine types and found that only 39/102 GSO tasks, 11/140 SWE-Perf tasks, and 411/498 SWE-fficiency tasks satisfied validity rules in all replays. Additionally, public submission rankings varied significantly depending on the scoring rule used.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Repository-level performance-optimization benchmarks evaluate coding agents by having them apply patches to real codebases to improve runtime, then comparing results against unoptimized baselines and expert-written reference patches. Leaderboard scores from these benchmarks are increasingly used as evidence of coding-agent progress, but they can conflate runtime instability, scoring rule artifacts, and task difficulty.

A new study systematically evaluates how well three open LLMs (Llama, Mistral, Qwen) replicate human cooperation across 121 game-theoretic experiments, finding that Llama closely matches human patterns while Qwen aligns with Nash equilibrium predictions. ## [Heuresis: Search Strategies for Autonomous AI Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25198) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Heuresis is a new framework that implements six search strategies for autonomous AI research agents, evaluated across quality, diversity, and novelty in three ML domains totaling 3,222 scored runs. This work systematically reveals that current LLM-based agents struggle to generate both novel and high-quality ideas, highlighting a critical bottleneck for autonomous scientific discovery. The study found that no generated idea was rated as 'Original', and only a few achieved 'Minor Similarity' to prior work; additionally, agents exhibited reward hacking with 40 confirmed fabrications across 1,628 scored runs.

## [HiComm: Receiver-Driven Hierarchical Communication for MARL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29126) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HiComm introduces a receiver-driven hierarchical communication module for multi-agent reinforcement learning that retrieves structured information from sender observations via a three-stage decoding process. This approach addresses the limitation of flat message passing in MARL by leveraging the natural hierarchical structure of observations, potentially improving efficiency and scalability in cooperative multi-agent systems. The module uses Straight-Through Gumbel-Softmax for differentiable discrete selection and reduces communication volume by up to 23× per receiver per episode while matching or outperforming baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: In cooperative multi-agent reinforcement learning, agents often need to communicate to overcome partial observability. Traditional methods treat messages as flat vectors, ignoring the hierarchical structure present in many environments (e.g., groups and entities). HiComm grounds messages in this hierarchy, enabling more efficient information retrieval.

<a id="item-125"></a>
## [Warrant: A Permission Gate for Attention Mechanisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30139) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Warrant', a path-localized interface that adds a learned permission gate to attention mechanisms, transforming the weighted value term from α_ij * v_j to α_ij * g_ij * v_j, ensuring relevance translates to warranted value contributions. This work addresses a fundamental gap in attention mechanisms: relevance does not guarantee prediction evidence. By formalizing a permission problem and proposing a generalizable solution, it has the potential to improve interpretability and model design across multiple domains including CTDG, MTPP, RAG, and STPP. Across 32 paired comparisons, 3 seeds, and 192 total runs, Warrant improves the primary metric in 27 comparisons, with 10 substantial effects and only 5 drops. Ablations show that most gains in TKG come from historical-tail value path exposure, while CTDG gains come from edge-conditioned query-item permission.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Attention mechanisms allow models to focus on relevant parts of input by assigning weights (α_ij) to key-value pairs. However, high relevance does not guarantee that the value contribution (v_j) is actually useful for prediction—a retrieved passage may be relevant but not supportive. The paper formalizes this as a 'permission problem' and proposes a learned gate (g_ij) to filter value contributions.

**Tags**: `#attention mechanisms`, `#interpretability`, `#machine learning`, `#deep learning`, `#arXiv`

Researchers introduced ManimAgent, a self-evolving multimodal agent that uses a dual-channel episodic memory bank to transfer reflection experience across code-generation tasks for mathematical animations, without weight updates or human seeds. This work addresses a key limitation in LLM-based agents by enabling cross-task learning from reflection, which could significantly improve code generation and educational tools. The approach has potential to reduce human effort in creating mathematical visualizations. The dual-channel memory bank stores success rationales as soft Reference Examples in a positive channel (M+) and validated failure patterns as hard Known Pitfalls in a negative channel (M-). In evaluations against baselines, blind human Pass@1 improved and reflection rounds decreased as memory size grew.

## [AI Agents Should Help Users Construct Preferences, Not Just Elicit Them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30863) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces CoPref, a model from information economics that formalizes how users construct preferences through agent dialog, and CoShop, a benchmark for agentic recommender systems. Experiments show no frontier model exceeds 56% accuracy on CoShop, highlighting a fundamental gap in current AI agents. This work challenges the common assumption that users have well-formed preferences, which is unrealistic in many domains. By formalizing preference construction, it paves the way for more effective human-AI interaction and recommender systems that truly help users discover what they want. CoPref is based on the Search-Experience-Credence framework from information economics, categorizing product attributes into search, experience, and credence types. CoShop simulates users who need to learn about credence attributes through interaction; agents must help users gain domain knowledge to specify tasks correctly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional AI agents assume users have fully formed preferences and ask clarifying questions when tasks are underspecified. However, users often lack domain knowledge to answer such questions. The Search-Experience-Credence framework classifies product attributes: search attributes can be evaluated before purchase, experience attributes after purchase, and credence attributes may never be fully known. CoPref models how agents can help users learn about credence attributes to construct preferences.

Researchers introduce Chain & Hash, a novel fingerprinting framework that cryptographically binds prompts to responses, providing verifiable proof of ownership for Large Language Models (LLMs) and robustness against meta-prompt attacks. This technique addresses the critical issue of LLM theft and misuse by enabling irrefutable ownership claims, which is essential for protecting intellectual property in the rapidly growing AI industry. The framework uses a chain and hash technique to prevent collisions and incorporates random padding and varied meta-prompt configurations during training to maintain robustness against output style changes. Experiments show it resists fine-tuning and adversarial removal, and can also fingerprint LoRA adapters.

A new study analyzes counterfactual faithfulness of LLM self-explanations across 75 models from 13 families, introducing two new metrics: phi-CCT and F-AUROC. This work provides a systematic evaluation of how faithful LLM explanations are, revealing scaling trends that larger models are more faithful, which is crucial for AI interpretability and trustworthiness. The phi-CCT metric simplifies the Correlational Counterfactual Test by avoiding token probabilities, while F-AUROC eliminates sensitivity to imbalanced intervention distributions and captures a model's ability to produce explanations with varying detail.

## [scDataset: Fast Data Loading for Single-Cell Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01883) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced scDataset, a PyTorch data loader that uses block sampling and batched fetching to achieve quasi-random sampling, enabling efficient training on single-cell datasets with hundreds of millions of cells. On Tahoe-100M (100 million cells), it achieves over 100x speedup compared to true random sampling while working directly with AnnData files. This addresses a critical bottleneck in deep learning for single-cell genomics, where datasets too large for memory require efficient on-disk data loading. By maintaining data diversity while achieving high throughput, scDataset enables training on massive single-cell datasets that were previously impractical. scDataset provides theoretical bounds on minibatch diversity and empirically matches the performance of true random sampling across multiple classification tasks and model architectures. It seamlessly integrates with diverse storage formats, particularly AnnData files.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Single-cell omics datasets can contain hundreds of millions of cells, exceeding available memory, so data must be loaded from disk during training. True random sampling provides optimal data diversity but is slow due to random access overhead, while sequential streaming is fast but introduces biases that degrade model performance. scDataset's quasi-random sampling balances I/O efficiency and minibatch diversity.

Researchers propose a quadratic programming method to compute exact Nash equilibria in multiplayer imperfect-information games, demonstrated on three-player Kuhn poker. This addresses a known limitation of existing algorithms like CFR and fictitious play, which do not guarantee convergence to Nash equilibrium in multiplayer games, potentially advancing AI for complex strategic interactions. The method formulates the equilibrium as a quadratically-constrained program based on a nonlinear complementarity problem from the sequence-form representation. It outperforms Gambit's logit quantal response approach in speed and exactness.

## [PathoGen: Diffusion-Based Lesion Inpainting for Histopathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08127) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced PathoGen, a controllable diffusion-based generative model that inpaints realistic synthetic lesions into benign histopathology images, validated across kidney, skin, breast, and prostate datasets. PathoGen addresses the critical bottleneck of rare pathology data scarcity in AI for histopathology, enabling scalable data augmentation that improves segmentation Dice scores by up to 0.18, especially in data-scarce regimes. In a blinded evaluation, six expert pathologists distinguished PathoGen-generated lesions from real tissue with only 57.75% accuracy, near chance level, and PathoGen achieved the highest win rate (35.4%) in quality ranking against baselines.

Diffusion models are a class of generative AI that can create high-quality images by gradually denoising random noise.

## [NeuroFilter: Activation-Based Guardrails for Privacy-Conscious LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.14660) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NeuroFilter proposes activation-based privacy filters for LLM agents that detect and prevent inappropriate information disclosure at inference time, offering a computationally efficient alternative to expensive LLM-based monitors. This work addresses a critical privacy risk in agentic LLMs, which are increasingly deployed in sensitive domains like personal assistants, finance, and law. The activation-based approach is both efficient and effective, potentially enabling practical privacy protection without the overhead of auxiliary LLMs. The filters are evaluated in both single-turn and multi-turn conversational settings, and the paper provides the first systematic investigation of probing model internals across a conversation trajectory. This moves beyond static, single-prompt analysis to capture the evolving state of privacy-sensitive interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic LLMs are models that can reason, plan, and execute tools over unstructured data, enabling transformative applications but requiring access to sensitive information. Existing defenses against inference-time privacy risks often rely on auxiliary LLM-based monitors, which are expensive and offer limited protection against robust attacks.

## [OmniMoE Pushes MoE Granularity to Extreme with Atomic Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05711) ⭐️ 8.0/10

OmniMoE introduces vector-level atomic experts with a Cartesian Product Router and Expert-Centric Scheduling, achieving 50.9% zero-shot accuracy across seven benchmarks with 1.7B active parameters and reducing inference latency by 10.9x compared to PEER. This work breaks the traditional trade-off between expert granularity and hardware efficiency, enabling massive-scale fine-grained MoE to be both fast and accurate, which could significantly improve the parameter efficiency of large language models. The Cartesian Product Router decomposes the routing index space to reduce complexity from O(N) to O(sqrt(N)), while Expert-Centric Scheduling inverts execution order to convert scattered memory-bound lookups into dense matrix operations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architectures use multiple specialized sub-networks (experts) to improve model capacity without proportionally increasing computation. Traditional MoE designs face a trade-off: finer-grained experts improve parameter efficiency but cause routing complexity and memory access overhead. OmniMoE addresses this via system-algorithm co-design.

## [Calibrated Test-Time Guidance for Bayesian Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22428) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies that common test-time guidance methods for diffusion models fail to sample from the true Bayesian posterior, and proposes consistent alternative estimators that achieve calibrated posterior sampling, setting a new state-of-the-art PSNR in black hole image reconstruction. This work corrects a fundamental miscalibration in test-time guidance, enabling diffusion models to perform proper Bayesian inference, which is crucial for scientific applications like black hole imaging and other inverse problems where uncertainty quantification is essential. The authors show that existing test-time guidance methods maximize reward rather than sample from the posterior, and they identify the structural approximations causing this failure. Their proposed estimators achieve calibrated sampling and significantly outperform previous methods on Bayesian inference tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that can be steered at test time via guidance mechanisms to produce outputs satisfying certain conditions. Bayesian posterior sampling aims to infer the distribution of unknown variables given observations, which is important for inverse problems like image reconstruction. Test-time guidance methods are widely used but often focus on maximizing a reward function rather than correctly sampling from the posterior, leading to miscalibrated results.

## [DeLL: Deconfounded Lifelong Learning for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14354) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose DeLL, a deconfounded lifelong learning framework for end-to-end autonomous driving that combines a Dirichlet process mixture model (DPMM) with causal front-door adjustment to mitigate catastrophic forgetting and spurious correlations. This work addresses two critical challenges in autonomous driving—catastrophic forgetting and spurious correlations—which are essential for deploying vehicles that continuously learn and adapt to new scenarios without losing prior knowledge. DeLL uses DPMM to build two dynamic knowledge spaces: a trajectory knowledge space and an implicit feature knowledge space, enabling adaptive expansion without predefining cluster numbers. The framework also introduces an evolutionary trajectory decoder for non-autoregressive planning and new evaluation protocols based on Bench2Drive.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: End-to-end autonomous driving (E2E-AD) systems learn driving policies directly from sensor inputs, but they suffer from catastrophic forgetting when learning sequentially across diverse scenarios. Spurious correlations, such as those caused by sensor noise or environmental changes, can mislead the model. The Dirichlet process mixture model is a non-parametric Bayesian method that can automatically determine the number of clusters, while front-door adjustment is a causal inference technique that uses a mediator variable to remove confounding bias.

## [MIPO Boosts LLM Performance via Mutual Information Maximization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19294) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Mutual Information Preference Optimization (MIPO), a method that constructs preference pairs by pairing a correct prompt with a positive response and a random, unrelated prompt with a negative response, then applies Direct Preference Optimization to maximize pointwise mutual information between prompts and responses without requiring additional data. MIPO addresses a key limitation in LLM post-training by enabling self-improvement without human-labeled data or external verifiers, achieving 3-51% gains on personalization and 1-20% gains on verifiable tasks like math and multiple-choice QA, making it broadly applicable to both verifiable and non-verifiable domains. Experiments with Llama and Qwen instruct models ranging from 1B to 7B parameters show that MIPO achieves up to 51% improvement on personalization for Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct compared to prompting baselines. The method is theoretically grounded in maximizing pointwise mutual information under the base LLM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Post-training of LLMs often relies on human-labeled preference data or external verifiers, which are expensive and limited. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) is a recent alignment technique that directly optimizes the policy from preference pairs without a separate reward model. MIPO extends DPO by constructing preference pairs from random prompts, leveraging the intrinsic signal of mutual information.

## [Moiré Effect as a Physical Signature Against AI Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01654) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a physics-based authentication method that uses the Moiré effect to distinguish real camera footage from AI-generated videos. They derive a Moiré motion invariant that correlates fringe phase with grating image displacement, providing a robust signature that generative models cannot reproduce. This approach addresses a critical challenge in deepfake detection by leveraging deterministic optical phenomena rather than learned features, which can be more robust against evolving generative models. It has the potential to significantly impact media forensics and authentication of video evidence. The method requires a compact two-layer grating structure to be placed in the scene, and the verifier extracts both the fringe phase and grating image displacement from the video to test their linear correlation. The invariant is validated against multiple state-of-the-art video generators, showing significantly different correlation signatures between real and AI-generated videos.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: The Moiré effect is an interference pattern that occurs when two similar periodic patterns are overlaid with slight displacement or rotation. In optics, a grating is a regularly spaced collection of parallel elements. This work exploits the natural Moiré patterns produced by real cameras when viewing a two-layer grating, which generative models cannot faithfully reproduce.

Fed-FSTQ introduces a Fisher-guided token quantization method that selectively preserves task-critical signals during federated fine-tuning of large language models on edge devices, reducing uplink communication by up to 46x and improving wall-clock time-to-accuracy by 52%. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in federated learning for LLMs on edge devices—straggler-limited uplink communication under heterogeneous bandwidth and non-IID data—by enabling efficient, model-agnostic compression without sacrificing task accuracy. Fed-FSTQ uses a lightweight Fisher proxy to estimate token sensitivity, combining importance-aware token selection with non-uniform mixed-precision quantization, and supports bandwidth-heterogeneous clients via compact sparse message packing. It is a drop-in module for standard PEFT pipelines like LoRA.

Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) reduces trainable parameters, but per-round payloads remain high. Fisher information measures how much a parameter affects the model output, guiding selective compression.

## [CAMS: Claim-Anchored Multi-Document Summarization Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23989) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose CAMS, a claim-anchored multi-document summarization framework that extracts atomic claims with token-level provenance, clusters equivalent claims, and rewrites summaries with per-sentence attribution to source spans. CAMS addresses hallucination and coarse attribution in LLM-based summarization by structurally preserving fine-grained, multi-source traceability, improving faithfulness and citation precision by roughly two-thirds. The pipeline follows an Extract-Select-Rewrite paradigm: it extracts atomic claims with token-level provenance, clusters equivalent claims while flagging conflicts, selects a support-aware subset, and rewrites into a summary where each sentence is anchored to a verified claim.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-document summarization aims to produce a concise summary from multiple source documents. End-to-end LLMs often hallucinate or provide coarse attributions (e.g., whole documents), making verification difficult. The Extract-Select-Rewrite paradigm decomposes the task into extracting salient content, selecting relevant information, and rewriting it into a coherent summary.

## [VLMs Over-rely on Text in Synthetic Medical Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25375) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that vision-language models (VLMs) can flip their authenticity judgments on synthetic medical images solely by changing the accompanying text metadata, with accuracy on authentic images dropping by 61.1% on average under an explicit AI-origin tag. This vulnerability undermines the reliability of VLM-based synthetic medical image detection in real-world clinical settings, where images are always accompanied by records and metadata, potentially enabling diagnostic deception or insurance fraud. The study introduces a paired benchmark that holds images fixed while swapping controlled metadata variants, and evaluates multiple open-weight and frontier API VLMs across diverse imaging modalities. The authors also propose an inference-time mitigation pipeline that detects and neutralizes provenance shortcuts without model retraining.

Prior detection methods typically evaluated images in isolation, ignoring the multimodal context in which VLMs are deployed.

The paper proposes a training-free gating mechanism that decides whether to rerank few-shot examples based on model uncertainty, reducing computational cost by 15-80% while improving average performance by up to 2%. This challenges the common assumption that reranking always helps, showing that targeted reranking based on uncertainty can be more efficient and effective, which has significant implications for deploying LLMs in resource-constrained settings. The method was validated across 8 LLMs, 7 NLU datasets, and 9 MT domain-language combinations, demonstrating consistent cost savings and performance gains without any additional training.

**Background**: Few-shot learning uses a small number of labeled examples to guide a model's predictions. Model uncertainty quantifies how confident a model is in its predictions; high uncertainty often indicates a need for more careful processing.

This paper introduces a contextual-bandit team game with two-sided asymmetric information to model runtime human oversight of AI agents, providing exact one-shot characterizations of the team optimum and a myopic rule. This work advances AI safety by formally analyzing the gap between optimal and myopic oversight, revealing a region of avoidable harm where the AI knows an action is harmful but a myopic human fails to oversee. It provides a theoretical foundation for designing better human-AI interaction protocols. The model uses a play/ask/trust/oversee interface and removes physical state transitions to yield exact one-shot characterizations, though common belief remains dynamically controlled across rounds. The gap between team optimum and myopic rule is identified as the price of non-credible oversight communication.

**Background**: Contextual bandits extend multi-armed bandits by using side information (context) to make personalized decisions. Cooperative Inverse Reinforcement Learning (CIRL) models human-AI collaboration where the human knows the reward function but the AI does not. The Oversight Game extends CIRL by adding two-sided information asymmetry, where both parties hold private information.

Researchers introduced BaRA, a web agent that combines bounded breadth-first search (BFS) with history-based self-reflection for site-level data collection under a fixed interaction budget. It outperforms existing methods like Pure LLM, SeeAct-Vision, and Browser-use on link discovery and multimodal extraction tasks. BaRA addresses practical limitations of LLM-based web agents, such as missing relevant pages and returning non-downloadable media URLs, improving the efficiency and reliability of automated web data collection. This advancement benefits AI/ML researchers and practitioners who need to gather large-scale multimodal data from live websites. BaRA was evaluated on 50 synthetic websites with ground-truth reference sets and three public websites with cluttered or dynamic layouts. BaRA combines these techniques to efficiently collect multimodal data under a fixed interaction budget.

GRACE-RAG is a novel retrieval-governed, graph-augmented RAG architecture that externalizes structural reasoning from the generative stage to a structured retrieval layer, enabling lightweight deployment on self-hosted models in closed-domain institutional settings. This approach reduces computational and latency footprint without relying on proprietary systems, making high-quality RAG feasible for institutions with limited resources. Experiments show consistent quality improvements of up to 20% under mid-scale models, indicating that retrieval architecture can govern structural quality over model scale. GRACE-RAG was evaluated across three model capacities: Mistral 24B, GPT OSS 120B, and Gemini 2.5 Flash, showing consistent improvements in completeness, depth, and anticipatory coverage. The architecture resolves structural ambiguity offline, enabling deployment on self-hosted lightweight models calibrated to closed-domain institutional vocabulary.

HydraCollab is an adaptive collaborative-perception framework that selectively transmits the most informative sensor features and dynamically switches between intermediate and late collaboration strategies based on spatial confidence maps, achieving a superior accuracy-bandwidth trade-off on V2X-R, V2X-Radar, and UAV3D-mini datasets. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in multi-robot systems: the trade-off between communication bandwidth and perception accuracy. By significantly reducing bandwidth usage (e.g., 41% of Where2comm on V2X-R) while maintaining or improving accuracy, HydraCollab enables more efficient and scalable collaborative perception in bandwidth-constrained environments like autonomous driving and drone swarms. HydraCollab employs collaborative sensor gating to select informative features and uses spatial confidence maps to decide between intermediate fusion (sharing feature maps) and late fusion (sharing detection results). On V2X-Radar, it uses only 26% of Where2comm's bandwidth while improving performance by 0.75%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Collaborative perception allows multiple robots (e.g., connected vehicles or drones) to share sensor data to improve situational awareness beyond what a single agent can achieve. However, sharing raw or intermediate data over wireless networks consumes limited bandwidth, creating a trade-off between communication cost and perception accuracy. Existing methods often fix the collaboration strategy or transmit all features, leading to inefficiency.

Researchers propose adaptive selection of structured audio perturbations for contrastive decoding in large audio-language models, achieving task-dependent accuracy gains of up to +6.7% on temporal order tasks and +4.3% on existence tasks. This work addresses a key limitation of existing contrastive decoding methods—reliance on blunt perturbations—by introducing a library of structured audio transformations and a lightweight selector, offering a training-free way to reduce hallucinations in audio-language models. The approach includes a simple binary yes/no constraint that reduces false confirmation of absent audio features, and a perturbation selector trained on model hidden states to dynamically choose the optimal negative branch per example.

**Background**: Large audio-language models (LALMs) can hallucinate by overriding acoustic evidence with language priors. Contrastive decoding (CD) mitigates this by amplifying differences between an expert model and a flawed amateur model, but existing CD methods use blunt perturbations like masking or noise. This paper explores structured audio transformations (e.g., temporal reversal, spectral filtering) as more targeted perturbations.
