## [Bias-Reliability Tradeoff Confirmed in LLM Evaluation Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00304) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new empirical study expands the evidence for the bias-reliability tradeoff in LLM evaluation from 5 to 11 conditions, measuring evaluator coupling (γ), strategy diversity (H), and small-sample reliability (CV). The results confirm that low coupling leads to high noise, while strong coupling suppresses diversity, with no condition achieving both low bias and high reliability. This work provides systematic empirical grounding for a fundamental constraint in LLM evaluation methodology, which is critical for AI safety and reliable model assessment. The findings suggest that practitioners must explicitly trade off between evaluator independence and measurement precision when designing evaluation pipelines. The study includes 11 conditions, with 9 having valid weight vectors and 7 having sufficient seeds for CV(N=5) calculation; only 5 conditions provide the complete (γ, H, CV) triple. Notably, four GPT-4o conditions exhibited γ=0.000 and H=1.000 across all seeds, attributed to version drift in the June 2026 GPT-4o API.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: LLM evaluation systems often use multiple evaluators (e.g., different prompts or models) to assess model outputs. The bias-reliability tradeoff conjectures that three key metrics—evaluator coupling (γ), strategy diversity (H), and small-sample reliability (CV)—cannot all be optimized simultaneously at a fixed sample size. Prior evidence was limited to 5 conditions from a single study; this work expands the empirical base to 11 conditions.

The paper introduces RetailSMV, a synchronized multi-view retail video dataset with 32,105 clips from five supermarkets, and compares parameter-efficient LoRA adaptation of the Cosmos3-Nano video world model using egocentric-only, exocentric-only, and combined training data. ## [DiscoLoop: Looping Discrete Embeddings and Continuous Hidden States for Multi-hop Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00341) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DiscoLoop introduces a looped Transformer architecture that carries both a discrete embedding channel and a continuous hidden-state channel, achieving near-perfect accuracy on multi-hop reasoning tasks with fewer training steps. This work addresses the depth-local storage problem in Transformers, a fundamental limitation for multi-hop reasoning, and demonstrates that a simple mixed-channel design can significantly improve reasoning capabilities without extra training. The authors found that in two-hop reasoning, the first loop often makes the correct bridge entity nearly perfectly decodable, but the hidden state remains poorly aligned with the bridge token embedding; a training-free realignment intervention nearly closes the generalization gap.

**Background**: Multi-hop reasoning requires a model to compose multiple pieces of knowledge within a single forward pass. Standard Transformers suffer from a depth-local storage problem where facts learned in earlier layers are unavailable for later retrieval. Looped Transformers reuse the same memory to mitigate this, but still generalize imperfectly due to representational bottlenecks.

## [Cross4D-JEPA Distills Dense 2D Features for 4D Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00514) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Cross4D-JEPA introduces a teacher-student framework that distills dense per-patch features from frozen 2D foundation models (DINOv2 or V-JEPA 2) into 4D point cloud encoders, using a per-point correspondence objective without masking, negatives, or decoder. This work addresses the gap in self-supervised 4D point cloud learning by leveraging rich 2D semantics, outperforming intra-modal and global cross-modal baselines on four benchmarks, and enabling better label efficiency and domain transfer, which is crucial for robotics and embodied perception. The method uses a dense cross-modal correspondence that maps each 3D point to its projected teacher patch feature, and a per-point objective to match these features in latent space. A 13x smaller encoder matches a heavyweight pooling backbone, and the dense representation improves full-label fine-tuning under the same training budget.

**Background**: 4D point clouds are sequences of 3D point clouds over time, captured by depth sensors or LiDAR. Previous methods either use intra-modal pretext tasks or distill only a single global embedding from 2D models, losing fine-grained semantics.

A user study with 24 participants found that a 15 percentage point improvement in task success rate (from 75% to 90%) in a multimodal human-robot interaction system was perceptible to users, with 70.83% preferring the improved system. This study bridges the gap between technical benchmarks and user experience in HRI, demonstrating that objective improvements can translate into subjective user preference, which is crucial for designing effective robotic systems. The baseline system used Whisper, Florence-2, LLaMA 3.1, and an interval Type-2 fuzzy logic controller; the improved system replaced perception and language modules with Grounding DINO + SAM and Qwen 3.5 9B. Perceptual constructs (speed, reliability, competence, fluency) were all rated significantly higher for the improved system with large effect sizes.

The paper introduces Compositional Primitive Fields (CPF-GCD), a framework that learns low-rank compositional representations to improve Generalized Category Discovery (GCD) by decomposing images into reusable visual primitives and their spatial layouts. This work addresses a key bottleneck in GCD—high-rank entangled token representations—by enforcing a low-rank compositional structure, which enables novel categories to emerge naturally. It can significantly boost open-world recognition tasks and serves as a plug-and-play module for existing GCD baselines. CPF is inserted between the backbone and the head, rewriting noisy patch tokens through low-rank primitive mixtures. It explicitly models spatial distribution of primitives, enabling novel categories to emerge as new activation patterns over a shared vocabulary.

## [Valdi: Value Diffusion World Models for Efficient MPC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00917) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Valdi introduces a value diffusion world model that enables model predictive control using a single diffusion step, matching deterministic baselines in the CarRacing environment. This work bridges diffusion models and real-time control, potentially enabling more expressive and uncertainty-aware planning in robotics and autonomous systems without sacrificing speed. The model jointly trains a latent diffusion dynamics model with objectives for representation learning, reward prediction, and value prediction in an online control loop, and the code is open-source on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: World models learn compressed representations of an environment to simulate future states, enabling planning algorithms like Model Predictive Control (MPC). Diffusion models can capture multimodal uncertainty but typically require many iterative denoising steps, making them too slow for real-time control. Valdi addresses this by using a single diffusion step while maintaining predictive quality.

## [Multi-Agent Framework for Long-Form Story Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00918) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MAGNET, a multi-agent goal-driven narrative engine, and ATLAS, a graph-based hallucination detection pipeline, achieving 41-50% reduction in annotations and hallucinations for 100-page narratives. This work addresses the key challenge of maintaining narrative consistency in long-form AI-generated stories, which is critical for applications in creative writing, game design, and interactive fiction. MAGNET uses persona-grounded character agents that propose actions based on a shared world state and evolving story goals, while ATLAS compares scene-level world representations to detect hallucinations. At 100 pages, MAGNET reduced annotations by 41% and hallucinations by 50% compared to single-model prompting.

## [GMHF: Human Feedback Guides Generative Meta-Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00926) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Generative Meta-Learning with Human Feedback (GMHF), a framework that uses expert intuition to guide data synthesis and reduce domain gap. It combines a Conditional Neural ODE (cNODE) as a generative digital twin with a Reinforcement Learning agent that refines latent parameters based on human feedback. GMHF addresses a critical challenge in machine learning: generalizing to unseen target domains when target data is unavailable. By incorporating human feedback, it provides a principled way to align generated data with expert beliefs, potentially improving robustness in real-world applications like robotics and scientific modeling. The framework is theoretically grounded with generalization error bounds showing that aligning generated data with human beliefs reduces risk. Empirical validation on a nonlinear Duffing oscillator demonstrates that deployment loss decreases as expert reliability increases, and divergence between generated and target data falls under reliable feedback.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Generative meta-learning aims to learn a model that can quickly adapt to new tasks by generating synthetic training data. However, a domain gap often exists between generated and real target data. Human feedback, as used in RLHF, can guide the generation process to better match expert expectations. Conditional Neural ODEs model continuous-time dynamics and can generate trajectories conditioned on latent parameters.

This paper compares four strategies for learning cardiac motion priors for implicit neural representations (INRs), showing that learned priors significantly improve early adaptation performance over random initialization. This work addresses the time-consuming and sensitive optimization of INRs for cardiac motion estimation, which is critical for medical imaging applications. The learned priors enable faster and more robust adaptation, potentially improving clinical workflows. The four strategies compared are a population prior learned by joint optimization, a consensus prior via weight averaging, auto-decoders, and meta-learning. Using short-axis tagged cardiac MRI from UK Biobank, meta-learning achieved strong early performance and maintained the best adaptation trajectory over 50 iterations.

DART-VLN introduces a training-free test-time control framework for discrete vision-language navigation, combining Test-Time Memory Decay and Anti-Loop Regularization to improve agent reliability without retraining. This work addresses common failure modes in memory-based VLN agents—stale memory and inefficient backtracking—with a lightweight, parameter-free solution that consistently improves performance on standard benchmarks, making it valuable for real-world deployment. The framework adds no new learnable parameters and leaves the frozen backbone unchanged; experiments on R2R and REVERIE show that decay-only provides stable gains, while decay+anti-loop achieves the best quality-efficiency trade-off with shorter trajectories and lower runtime.

Researchers propose a language-critique framework for imitation learning that uses natural language as structured supervision, providing fine-grained corrective guidance from suboptimal demonstrations. They introduce LC-BC and LC-DP methods, which outperform strong baselines in continuous control tasks. This approach addresses the limitation of scalar supervision signals (e.g., confidence estimates) that cannot express intermediate reasoning, potentially enabling more robust policy learning from imperfect data. It could reduce the need for expert demonstrations in robotics and autonomous systems. The framework constructs language labels describing progress, suboptimal behaviors, and corrective actions, then uses a language-critique loss to train policies without reducing feedback to scalars. Theoretical results show the objective upper-bounds the expert performance gap under standard assumptions.

CoT-X is an adaptive framework that compresses chain-of-thought reasoning traces via semantic segmentation, budget-aware dynamic compression, and coherence reconstruction, achieving up to 40% higher accuracy than truncation under the same token budgets across diverse LLMs. This work addresses the critical inference overhead of chain-of-thought reasoning, enabling efficient deployment of advanced reasoning in resource-constrained settings. Evaluations on 64 model pairs from eight LLMs (1.5B-32B parameters, including DeepSeek-R1 and Qwen3) confirm strong cross-model transferability, and a Gaussian Process-based Bayesian optimization module reduces evaluation cost by 84%.

Researchers propose an Explicit Logic Channel (ELC) that runs parallel to black-box MLLMs, performing explicit logical reasoning to validate, select, and enhance model outputs on zero-shot tasks. This work addresses a critical gap in deploying MLLMs for zero-shot tasks by providing a method to validate and enhance black-box models with explicit reasoning, improving trustworthiness and explainability. The ELC combines an LLM, a VFM, and probabilistic logical reasoning for factual, counterfactual, and relational reasoning over explicit visual evidence. This paper presents the first systematic comparison of Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) and Soft Actor-Critic (SAC) for integrating fault tolerance into control systems, evaluating four knowledge-transfer strategies in Ant-v5 and FetchReachDense-v3 environments. This research demonstrates that reinforcement learning can deliver robust fault tolerance, offering practical guidelines for deploying RL in autonomous machines that must adapt to hardware faults without redundant hardware. ## [Benchmark of Lightweight CNNs for Edge Devices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.03303) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A reproducible benchmark compares seven lightweight CNNs on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny ImageNet, showing EfficientNetV2-S achieves top accuracy while EfficientNet-B0 offers the best efficiency-accuracy trade-off. This study provides a fair comparison of lightweight CNNs under a unified protocol, helping practitioners select models for resource-constrained devices like smartphones and IoT sensors. EfficientNet-B0 uses only 21% of the parameters and 14% of the multiply-accumulate operations of EfficientNetV2-S on Tiny ImageNet while staying within 0.85 percentage points in accuracy. MobileNetV3-Small is highlighted for ultra-low-resource settings, using about 40% of the parameters and 15% of the MACs of EfficientNet-B0.

**Background**: Lightweight convolutional neural networks (CNNs) are designed for deployment on devices with limited compute, memory, and power, such as mobile phones and embedded systems. This benchmark uses a common fine-tuning protocol to eliminate confounding factors from different training recipes.

## [Flow-Through Tensors Unify Multi-Layer Transport Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.02961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Flow-Through Tensors (FTT) is a new computational graph architecture that connects origin-destination flows, path probabilities, and link travel times as interconnected tensors, enabling gradient-based optimization across previously separate modeling elements. This framework bridges the gap between theoretical transportation models and practical deployment, enabling real-time control, multi-modal coordination, and enforcement of physical constraints, which could significantly improve the efficiency of next-generation integrated mobility systems. The framework supports multidimensional analysis over time, space, and user groups, and uses tensor decomposition techniques to maintain computational tractability for large-scale applications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Transportation network modeling traditionally involves separate methods for demand forecasting, path choice, and traffic assignment, which are often optimized independently. Computational graphs and tensor decompositions are techniques from machine learning that allow unified representation and gradient-based optimization of complex systems. This paper applies these concepts to transportation, creating a single differentiable model.

This work addresses the critical issue of overconfident classifiers and poor latent separation in synthetic oversampling for class imbalance, achieving superior F1-score (93.74%) and recall (92.85%), which could significantly enhance real-world fraud detection systems. The method was compared against SMOTE and state-of-the-art generative models, with ablation studies confirming the benefits of classifier-driven representation learning.

## [TANDEM: Attention-Guided Neural ODEs for Missing Time Series](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17519) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose TANDEM, a framework that integrates attention mechanisms with neural ordinary differential equations (ODEs) to classify time series data with missing values, outperforming state-of-the-art methods on 30 benchmarks and a real medical dataset. Missing data is a pervasive challenge in time series analysis across healthcare, finance, and IoT; TANDEM offers a principled way to handle missingness without separate imputation, potentially improving accuracy and reliability in critical applications. TANDEM combines raw observations, an interpolated control path, and continuous latent dynamics via a novel attention mechanism that focuses on the most informative data aspects. The model was evaluated on 30 benchmark datasets and a real-world medical dataset, showing significant improvement over existing methods.

## [FlowPath: Invertible Flows for Robust Irregular Time Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.10841) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FlowPath introduces a novel approach that learns data-adaptive control paths via invertible neural flows for irregularly-sampled time series classification, outperforming fixed interpolation methods on 18 benchmark datasets. This work addresses a key limitation of neural controlled differential equations (CDEs), which are sensitive to the choice of control path; FlowPath's invertibility constraint ensures information-preserving transformations, leading to more robust and generalizable classification. FlowPath constructs a continuous and data-adaptive manifold guided by invertibility constraints, distinguishing it from prior unconstrained learnable path models. Empirical evaluations on 18 benchmark datasets and a real-world case study show statistically significant improvements in classification accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Neural controlled differential equations (CDEs) are a framework for modeling continuous-time dynamics from sparse and irregularly-sampled time series. They rely on a control path constructed from discrete observations, typically using fixed interpolation schemes like linear or cubic splines, which can misrepresent the underlying data manifold under high missingness. Invertible neural flows, also known as normalizing flows, are a class of deep learning models that learn bijective transformations with tractable Jacobians, enabling exact likelihood estimation and information-preserving mappings.

Researchers introduced KAGE-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating visual generalization in reinforcement learning, along with KAGE-Env, a JAX-native 2D platformer that factorizes visual shifts into independent axes. The benchmark includes 34 train-evaluation configuration pairs across six known-axis suites. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in RL research by enabling systematic isolation of visual distribution shifts, which often cause failures in pixel-based agents. Using a standard PPO-CNN baseline, the study found that background and photometric shifts often cause catastrophic failures, while agent-appearance shifts are relatively benign. Some shifts preserve forward motion but break task completion, showing that return alone can mask generalization failures.

## [Cue-Conflict Benchmark Flawed; REFINED-BIAS Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10834) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper identifies significant reliability issues in the cue-conflict benchmark for measuring shape-texture bias in neural networks and introduces REFINED-BIAS, a new evaluation framework that uses balanced cue pairs and a ranking-based metric for more reliable bias diagnosis. This work is significant because the cue-conflict benchmark is widely used in interpretability and robustness research, and its flaws could lead to misleading conclusions about model behavior. REFINED-BIAS offers a more rigorous method for comparing shape-texture bias across models, potentially improving the fairness and reproducibility of vision model evaluations. The paper identifies three main issues with the cue-conflict benchmark: stylization may not produce perceptually valid cues, ratio-based bias can obscure absolute cue sensitivity, and restricting evaluation to preselected classes distorts predictions. REFINED-BIAS addresses these by constructing balanced cue pairs with explicit shape and texture definitions and using a ranking-based metric over the full label space.

**Background**: The cue-conflict benchmark is a standard method for measuring whether neural networks rely more on shape or texture cues, inspired by human visual perception. ## [EgoSim: Closed-Loop Egocentric World Simulator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01001) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EgoSim introduces a closed-loop egocentric world simulator that generates spatially consistent interaction videos by modeling 3D scenes as updatable world states, overcoming limitations of static or ungrounded simulators. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in egocentric simulation for embodied AI, enabling realistic multi-stage interactions and cross-embodiment transfer to robotic manipulation, which could accelerate research in robotics and human-computer interaction. EgoSim uses a Geometry-action-aware Observation Simulation model for interaction generation and an Interaction-aware State Updating module for spatial consistency. It also introduces EgoCap, a low-cost capture system using uncalibrated smartphones, and a scalable pipeline to extract training data from in-the-wild monocular videos.

## [Hardening x402: PII-Safe Agentic Payments via Pre-Execution Metadata Filtering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11430) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced presidio-hardened-x402, the first open-source middleware that intercepts x402 payment requests to detect and redact personally identifiable information (PII) before transmission, enforce spending policies, and block replay attacks. This addresses a critical privacy gap in AI agent payments, where payment metadata containing PII is exposed to untrusted servers. The solution enables safer autonomous transactions, which is essential as AI agents increasingly handle financial operations. The recommended configuration (NLP mode, min_score=0.4, all entity types) achieves micro-F1=0.894 with precision 0.972, at p99 latency of 5.73ms—well within the 50ms overhead budget. The middleware, synthetic corpus of 2,000 x402 metadata triples, and experiment code are publicly available on GitHub.

## [Rock Tokens: Persistent High-Loss Tokens in On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09253) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper discovers that high-loss tokens, termed 'Rock Tokens', persist in On-Policy Distillation even after training converges, contradicting prior expectations that such tokens would diminish. These tokens can account for up to 18% of generated tokens and contribute disproportionately to gradient norms yet remain stagnant and functionally negligible. This finding challenges the necessity of uniform token weighting in distillation, suggesting that optimization bandwidth is wasted on structural and discourse residuals. Strategically bypassing Rock Tokens could streamline alignment and improve efficiency in large-scale model distillation. Rock Tokens are identified through per-token KL divergence analysis in On-Policy Distillation. Despite providing a large share of gradient norms, they resist teacher-driven corrections and contribute negligibly to reasoning performance, as shown by causal intervention experiments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: On-Policy Distillation (OPD) is a technique where a student model learns from a teacher model using on-policy samples and per-token KL divergence, avoiding exposure bias. Previous work in RLVR highlighted critical tokens driving reasoning gains, but token-level analysis in OPD was unexplored. This paper fills that gap by examining high-loss tokens.

PSCT-Net introduces a geometry-aware deep learning framework that reconstructs 3D pediatric skull CT from sparse bi-planar X-rays using differentiable back-projection and attention-guided refinement, reducing radiation exposure. The framework includes a differentiable back-projection module for spatially faithful volumetric priors, an Attention-Guided Projection (AGP-3D) module for non-linear 2D-3D correspondences, and a Bidirectional Mamba (BiM-3D) module for efficient long-range volumetric dependencies. ## [Topological Neural Dynamics: Neuron-wise Sequence Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21295) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Topological Neural Dynamics (TND) is introduced, a sequence modeling framework that replaces traditional layer-wise dynamics with neuron-wise dynamics, where each neuron evolves independently via a directed graph. This shift from layer-wise to neuron-wise dynamics provides a new inductive bias that could lead to more expressive and efficient sequence models, potentially outperforming RNNs, LSTMs, and Transformers on tasks requiring complex temporal dependencies. TND represents a neural system as a directed neuron graph, an interaction operator, and a local dynamics function. In a behavior cloning task on single-player Pong, TND achieved a mean of 17.47 consecutive catches per round, more than three times the strongest baseline.

**Background**: Existing sequence models like RNNs, LSTMs, and Transformers operate on layer-wise dynamics, where all neurons in a layer share the same parameterized operator. In contrast, many complex systems exhibit emergent behavior from locally evolving units interacting through structured connectivity. TND draws inspiration from this principle to model sequences at the neuron level.

## [Tighter Variable Bounds Speed Nash Equilibrium in Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25997) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper derives finite bounds on slack and multiplier variables in a quadratically constrained program formulation for exact Nash equilibrium computation in multiplayer imperfect-information games, which strengthens convex relaxations in spatial branch-and-bound and leads to substantial computational improvements. Exact Nash equilibrium computation in multiplayer imperfect-information games is a long-standing challenge in game theory and AI, as regret-based methods do not guarantee convergence. This work makes exact computation more practical, potentially enabling better analysis of complex strategic interactions. The proposed bounds are applied to three-player Kuhn poker, where the algorithm previously could not solve the full game within 24 hours; the new bounds enable faster convergence. The approach uses Gurobi's nonconvex quadratic solver with spatial branch-and-bound and McCormick envelopes for bilinear terms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: In multiplayer imperfect-information games, players have private information and act simultaneously or sequentially without full knowledge of others' actions. Computing Nash equilibria exactly is NP-hard in general, and traditional regret-based methods like counterfactual regret minimization only guarantee convergence in two-player zero-sum games. The sequence-form representation encodes game strategies efficiently, and the nonlinear complementarity problem formulation allows exact equilibrium computation via quadratically constrained programs.

This paper introduces a dual-gate diagnostic pipeline for certified robustness in automatic speech recognition, achieving up to 55% relative reduction in Word Error Rate across four architectures. This work addresses the critical challenge of detecting and mitigating adversarial and benign perturbations in deployed ASR systems, which lack oracle knowledge of true transcriptions. The certification-inspired mechanism provides granular word- and sentence-level guarantees, enhancing acoustic security for real-world applications. The pipeline consists of a Two-Sided Atomic Audit that accumulates statistical wealth to certify token existence and adversarial exclusion, and a Rank-Based Tournament that selects the winning sequence. Evaluations across four diverse architectures demonstrate up to 55% relative WER reduction while providing certifications.

This architecture addresses a critical gap in LLM deployment for regulated industries by enforcing data residency compliance by design, preventing PII from reaching unauthorized endpoints, and reducing costs by routing simple queries to smaller models. It targets Australian regulations SEPP65, BADS, and SPP7.3.

<a id="item-291"></a>
## [DLSL Systems: Characterization and Realization Cost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00044) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces destination-labeled self-looping systems with dwell (DLSL systems) and characterizes their realizations as fiber-linear graph-respecting transducers, providing an O(|Q||Ω|) recognition and reconstruction procedure. This work provides a rigorous theoretical foundation for symbolic controllers with dwell constraints, which is crucial for safety-critical systems where actions must respect minimum time requirements. The results enable systematic design and verification of such controllers. The paper proves that any graph-preserving deterministic realization enforcing dwell values (d_i) requires exactly ∑ d_i control states. It also extends the analysis to an edge-entry variant where transitions may enter interior phases of successor fibers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 2, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic controllers are finite-state models used in control theory to enforce safety and liveness properties. Dwell constraints require the system to stay in a state for a minimum duration before leaving, which is common in real-time systems. Transducers are automata with output, and fiber-linear graph-respecting transducers are a specific class that respects the graph structure and linearity in fibers.

The framework integrates a learnable text-guided diffusion model to generate realistic tail-class samples, a channel reweighting module for feature recalibration, and a graph convolution network to model label co-occurrence. Experiments on PadChest show an overall mAP of 0.4408 and mAUC of 0.8989.

This work clarifies the acoustic cues used by distance estimation models, which can guide the design of more robust systems for applications like audio surveillance, teleconferencing, and virtual reality. Without time calibration, the mean absolute error (MAE) increases to 1.29 m, and the model relies on reverberation-based cues, with early reflections being the most informative component; with time calibration, the model achieves 0.14 m MAE by extracting propagation delay alone.
